金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 19 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 9 月通膨升溫，Fed 年底前再升息機率仍高： 美國 9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，高於 8 月的
3.4%；核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.4%升至 2.6%，顯示通膨壓力不只來自能源，核心價格亦有回
升。隔日的 PPI 年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%，反映上游成本壓力仍強。通膨重新升溫，使Fed
即使面對就業降溫，也難以快速轉向寬鬆，市場對年底前再度升息的預期仍高。
◼ 台積電財報創新高，AI 需求強勁但市場轉向檢視融資風險：台積電 10/15 公布其第三季淨利

達 7,631 億元、年增 69%，連續第六季刷新紀錄，第三季美元營收 467.2 億美元，並預估第
四季營收將升至 523～543 億美元 ； 公司同時將2026 全年營收成長預估提高至約 44%， 反映
AI 晶片與先進製程需求仍極為強勁。然而，高利率環境使市場開始檢視 AI 資料中心業者的槓
桿與融資能力，CoreWeave 約 500 億美元債務引發信用疑慮，引發市場波動。
◼ 中東衝突再度推高能源風險 ， 油價高檔加劇全球通膨壓力 ：胡塞武裝上週攻擊沙烏地阿美設施 ，
伊朗亦表示採取新的海上攻擊策略，使荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣航運風險再度升高。10/16 布蘭特
原油與西德洲原油期貨雙雙收在近三週高點。
市場展望
◼ 科技股中期趨勢料將不變 ， 但短線波動勢將加劇 ：近期市場同時面臨通膨升溫 、AI 資本支出疑
慮與重要科技財報公佈在即等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能受到壓抑。在利率維持高檔及市
場評價偏高的環境下，市場短線料將隨財報結果與市場預期而出現較大波動。然而，上週台積
電與 ASML 財報結果整體優於預期 ， 可見AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡象 ，

科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，惟後續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出變動及科技子
產業的表現分化現象。
◼ 中天期美債與投資級債債券兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性 ， 加上政府與企業發債供
給維持高檔，可能使市場利率持續處於較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企
業獲利、現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，加上較高的美國公債基準利率，使投資級債的利息收益
仍具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較容易
受到公債供給增加 、 期限溢酬及利率波動影響 ， 因此中天期美國公債與投資債券較能兼顧息收
與利率風險。

2026年金融街论坛学习体会

货币政策传导效率进一步提升   
主要政策利率即将换锚 。贷款利率定价基准趋向多元化。进一步出台稳市举
措。支持 AI 发展，推进 AI 应用。 
 10 月 19 日，2026 金融街论坛年会正式开幕，年会主题为“开放・合作・
包容——共筑全球金融发展新生态”。 
 主要政策利率 即将换锚。中国人民银行行长潘功胜发表题为《 关于当前
市场关注若干问题的思考 》的主题演讲。潘行长 在演讲中指出，DR001
“作为货币市场交易活跃、最具基准性的利率，能够灵敏反映短期资金
供求状况”，已成为央行货币政策调控的目标利率。 
考虑到当前央行公开市场操作的主要品种及利率期限均仍为 7 天，潘行
长指出，央行“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为
隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率 ”。此次调整将
主要政策利率与目标利率 的期限对齐（均为隔夜） ，将央行日常流动性
管理精准至 单日，短端利率调控机制 得到有效优化， 有助于提升利率传
导效率。 
从我国利率走廊建设的角度看，2024 年央行创设临时隔夜正/逆回购工具，
利率为 7 天逆回购利率−20bp / +50bp，作为我国利率走廊边界。2026 年 6
月央行进一步优化利率走廊，将临时隔夜正/逆回购工具利率调整为 7 天
逆回购利率−20bp / + 20bp，但利率走廊操作基准利率仍为 7 天逆回购利
率。央行此次将公开市场主要操作品种期限切换 至隔夜，完成了基准利
率、走廊边界及目标利率期限 的对齐，有望进一步提升我国流动性管理
政策的精准度，平缓未来利率波动。 
 贷款利率定价基准 趋向多元化。潘行长在讲话中指出， 未来“财务能力
强的企业”可在 DR001 和 LPR 之间自主选择浮动利率贷款定价基准，LPR
不再是贷款 利率的单一基准，一定程度上赋予了企业、银行 在信贷活动
中定价基准的选择权。这一举措或能在 疏通央行政策传导渠道的同时，
降低实体融资成本。当前我国 LPR 报价按月公布，DR001 则为日频成交
价格，后者对银行间资金成本反映更加充分；以 DR001 为贷款利率定价
基准，或能 使贷款价格与银行间流动性 水平进一步对齐 ，在银行间流动
性宽松的情况下，推动“财务能力强的企业”融资成本下降。 
 进一步出台 稳市举措。潘行长在讲话中 提出“设立应对非银机构大额赎
回的流动性支持工具 ”，旨在进一步 建立债券市场稳定机制。此外，证
监会主席吴清也在讲话中提出 “加强风险研判和政策工具储备，巩固完
善稳市机制建设 ”，“扩大中长期资金来源、渠道和入市方式 ”，“壮
大市场稳定力量”。 
 支持 AI 发展，推进 AI 应用。潘行长指出央行要做好三方面工作：“密
切跟踪研判人工智能发展的宏观影响 ”；“做好金融支持人工智能产业
发展工作”；“稳妥推进人工智能在金融领域的应用” ； 并特别提出“完
善人工智能企业融资支持体系 ”，金融对科技创新的支持强度有望进一
步提升。在推进 AI 应用方面，吴清主席也在讲话中指出将“发布实施人
工智能在证券期货业安全合规应用的意见”，并促进 AI“在风控、投研
等重点场景安全高效应用”。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

1 
2408 南亞科(Nanya Technology)

報告日期： 2026/10/13 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 毛利率優於預期且將維持 8 成以上 
 2027 年 DRAM 供需仍緊俏 
 新廠 2027 年第四季加入貢獻

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

二. 4Q26 整體產業仍將維持供不應求態勢  
受惠於 AI 需求 ， AI 運算（GPU 、 TPU 、 CPU 及 ASIC）成為 DRAM 
需求成長主力，帶動高容量、高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求，
範疇從 HBM 、LPDDR5 進一步擴展至網路、儲存與系統管理等領
域。在傳統應用市場方面，由於 DRAM 供給不足與 ASP 上漲，
部分傳統應用終端出現產品降規或延長生命週期的現象，低階市
場則有回收 DRAM 支撐部分需求。供給端方面，因新廠建置、設
備交期、製程導入均需要較長的前置時間，目前供給仍維持緊俏，
智慧型手機、個人電腦及一般消費性記憶體供給受限，製造商與
供應鏈端的庫存水位普遍偏低 ， 隨著供應商依 LTA 長約進行產品
組合與產能規劃，有利未來供需穩定度。

三. 新廠預估 4Q27 開始投產 
公司 5A 新廠預計 2027 年第一季裝機，2027 年第四季開始投
產 ， 預計 2028 年底前達到每月投片量 3 萬片 ， 全產能目標於 2030 
年底前達每月 5 萬片，未來產能擴充均以客戶長期合約的 AI 專
案為主要目標。另外公司亦佈局先進封測領域，規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠 ， 預估 2H28 設備進駐 。 公司 2026
年前三季累計資本支出為 276 億元 ， 2026 年資本支出以不超過 697
億元為上限，其中生產設備資本支出約占五成。

四.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 元/ 134.3 元 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：670 元 
       5 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：527.0 元 
     3.92 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/12 收盤

2 
展望 2026 下半年至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大 ， 接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 ， 小型
語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記
憶體需求持續上揚。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅增加產能，預估
2027 年新增產能有限，多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新
增產能實際貢獻要到 2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科新廠預計 2028 年放
量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，全產能將達 5 萬片/月。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 670 元 
南亞科為全球前五大 DRAM 廠，受惠於 AI 帶動之供給端改善，記
憶體產品漲價快速，南亞科今明年獲利創高，2028 年全球雖然有
新產能開出，但隨著 AI 由模型訓練與推理延伸至更多客製化邊緣
AI 應用，帶動記憶體位元需求長期成長。考量到 AI 應用滲透率提
升帶動記憶體需求，記憶體已成戰略物資，客戶亦開始簽訂長約，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 670 元，建議買進

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1 995 年，為 DRAM （動態隨機存取記憶體）之研
發、設計、製造與銷售商，為全球第四大 DRAM 廠，市佔率約 
3%，由美光授權製程技術，其十二吋晶圓廠位於泰山南林園區，
月產能 6.8 萬片 ， 其中 20nm 製程約 3.8 萬片 ， 30nm 製程約 3 萬
片，公司產品廣泛應用於手機、PC、消費性電子產品，主要競爭
對手包括 SK Hynix、三星電子、美光、華邦電等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動記憶體價格雙位數上漲 
全球 CSP 大廠持續加強投資 AI server 及相關基礎建設，全球九大
CSP 廠之資本支出持續高速成長，2026 年資本支出將達 9220 億元
(YoY+98%)，2027 年資本支出成長至 13830 億美元(YoY+50%)。
TrendForce 預估 2026 年 HBM 需求量年增 70%。而隨著 AI 應用由
LLM 模型訓練延伸至推論，CSP 廠擴大通用型伺服器布建，也使
記憶體拉貨重心由 HBM 延伸至 RDIMM ， 進一步推升 DDR5 價格。
3Q26 在 AI 與資料中心建置需求持續下記憶體仍嚴重供不應求 ， 儘
管價格基期已高，但預估 DRAM 合約價持續上漲，TrendForce 預
估第三季整體 conventional DRAM 合約價上漲 13-18% ， 第四季持續
上漲 10%~15%。供給方面，DRAM 三大原廠今年新增產能有限，
三星及海力士均以既有廠區調整及製程轉換為主，預估晶圓產能
分別增加 5%及 8%，而美光產能持平，三大原廠新廠產能開出並

明顯貢獻預估在 2028 年 。 預估 2026 年 DRAM 價格逐季調升 ， 2027
年新增供給有限下，價格易漲難跌。

2. 2027 年記憶體需求維持強勁 
展望 2026 第四季至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大，接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 (如: 
Edge、客製化)，小型語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置 (PC、手機、汽
車、機器人等)，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記憶體需求持續
上揚。需求面依各應用別而言，Server 方面，AI 及高效能運算持
續驅動 HBM 與 DDR5、LPDDR5 需求，高階 e-SSD、BMC、NIC
對 DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求顯著; PC 及手機方面，高階 PC
與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定 ， 然中低階需求疲弱; 消
費型電子市場方面，AI 及一般伺服器對 HBM 、RDIMM 需求持續
擴張，排擠一般消費型記憶體供給。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅
增加產能，聚焦於高階雲端與高附加價值產品需求，DRAM 大廠
優先分配產能於 DDR5 及 HBM 產品 ， 預估 2027 年新增產能有限，
多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新增產能實際貢獻要到
2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科第二代 10 奈米級製程(1B) 16Gb DDR5 產
品交運量已達 10%，第三代 10 奈米級(1C)及第四代 10 奈米級(1D)
製程技術試產中。公司 DDR5 營收約占 10%，並可視需求彈性調
整增加，同時持續供應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 產品以緩解市場缺口。
公司亦積極與策略夥伴合作拓展 AI 邊緣運算驅動的記憶體市場，
目前處工程驗證階段，預計於 2026 年底推出新產品。南亞科 2025
年資本支出 134 億元，2026 年資本支出預計不超過 697 億元，其
中生產設備支出約占五成，新廠預計於 2027 年第一季開始裝機，
2027 年第四季開始生產，2028 年放量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，
預估 2030 年全產能將達 5 萬片/月。南亞科 2025 年位元銷售量成
長 50 多%，預估 2026 年成長 17~19%，預估 2027 年成長中低個位
數。

出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合

期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

New capacity announcements do not overturn our bullish view.
AI demand and increasing substrate complexity should keep the
market undersupplied from 2027, supporting further pricing and
margin expansion. We raise earnings estimates and price targets
for Unimicron and NYPCB and reiterate OW on both.
More capacity, but limited near-term relief: Suppliers are stepping up investment,

but several newly announced projects are not scheduled to ramp until late 2028-30
or beyond. Our updated supply-demand model continues to indicate undersupply
from 2027 through 2030, with tighter supply in CY27-28, which should only be
more favorable to pricing. Therefore, capacity additions announced in the past few
months do not, in our view, diminish the magnitude of pricing gains over the next
two years.
CY27 pricing should accelerate, amid tighter supply-demand in CY27-28, but

visibility on the magnitude remains limited: We see ABF substrate prices rising
25%+ in CY27, below buy-side expectations of ~50%. Our supply/demand outlook
points to undersupply next year, with larger substrates, higher layer counts, and
greater manufacturing complexity further constraining usable output. Raw material
cost pass-through was a key driver of CY26 price increases, we expect supply
tightness to play a greater role in CY27. This should allow pricing to outpace cost

inflation, driving faster margin expansion. We remain constructive on pricing, but the
magnitude and timing of increases depends on customer-level negotiations.
With pricing a segment-wide tailwind, we prefer Unimicron for its stronger CY27
capacity growth: As supply tightens, the ability to deliver incremental volume
should become a key differentiator. We favor suppliers bringing capacity online in
CY27 over those whose expansion plans contribute in later years. Unimicron’s

Guangfu II ramp should add 10-15% to capacity in CY27, compared with 5-10%
growth at NYPCB through debottlenecking, giving Unimicron greater scope to
benefit from both pricing and volume growth. Unimicron is also our preferred play
heading into earnings, as more demanding expectations for NYPCB leave less room
for an upside surprise. But we remain OW on NYPCB given its networking and AMD
CPU exposure, alongside its proactive pricing strategy.
We reiterate Overweight on Unimicron and NYPCB: Reflecting stronger earnings

trajectories, we raise our EPS estimates for both companies. Based on upside to our
revised price targets, our order of preference is Unimicron (NT$1,775 PT; 24x CY28e
P/E; 41% upside), followed by NYPCB (NT$2,000 PT; 22x CY28e P/E; 39% upside).
Potential risks: 1) Weaker demand for PCs, general servers, and AI servers; 2)
meaningful capacity expansion plans; 3) T-glass shortage; 4) CoWoP displacing ABF
substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 14, 2026 08:00 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Supply/Demand Update: Undersupply Deepens in CY27–28
Our updated model points to a wider supply shortfall in CY27–28, despite additional
capacity: Incorporating the latest expansion plans and semiconductor demand forecasts,
we now expect ABF substrate demand to exceed available supply by 9.3% in CY27 and
13.7% in CY28, versus 6.2% and 9.4% previously. This represents a widening of 3.1ppt and
4.3ppt, respectively, relative to our prior estimates. We maintain our view that CY26 is a

broadly balanced market, followed by increasingly tight supply over the next two years.
The main demand upgrade comes from other ASICs, including Chinese AI chips: We
raise our shipment assumptions for this category to 6.4mn units in CY27 and 8.7mn in
CY28, from 4.6mn and 6.2mn previously. Our Nvidia GPU and server CPU forecasts remain
largely unchanged, while we lower AMD GPU estimates while also keeping CY27 Trainium
and TPU shipments largely unchanged. The tighter substrate outlook therefore reflects

stronger demand across a broader set of ASIC suppliers, rather than higher shipment
assumptions for every major AI platform. We also raise our substrate content assumptions
for select chips based on our latest supply chain checks, lifting our substrate demand
estimates across some segments, including server CPUs and networking.
Higher supply assumptions provide limited relief in CY27-28: We now include additional
capacity announcements that have been announced recently, including two additional

Kinsus factories, scheduled to come online in CY28 and CY29, beyond the CY27 expansion
already in our model. Several larger projects, however, contribute later. NYPCB’s new site
starts ramping in 2H29, while SEMCO’s Sejong expansion begins mass production in
September 2028 and its project in Vietnam is scheduled to begin operations by June
2030. These additions increase medium-term supply but do not prevent the modeled
shortfall from worsening through CY28.

Increasing substrate complexity adds to the pressure on usable output: Larger body
sizes, higher layer counts, and more demanding manufacturing processes constrain the
output available to meet demand, even as suppliers expand installed capacity. With the
supply gap widening in CY27-28, we expect tightness to become a more important pricing
driver than raw material cost pass-through. This supports our forecast for 25%+ price
increases in CY27 and further increases in CY28, alongside continued margin expansion.

NYPCB (8046.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On August 6, NYPCB announced that its Board of Directors had approved a capex budget
of NT$46.8bn (~US$1,474mn) to invest in building a new ABF substrate production site.
Our checks indicate that this production site is located in the southern part of Taiwan, and
will start to ramp up production in 2H29. Given the size of this investment, we estimate
that it will approximately double its capacity exiting CY30, relative to its CY28 capacity.

Kinsus (3189.TW, Not Covered)
In our previous model, we had assumed only one factory (k6b) coming online in CY27,
increasing capacity ~30%. However, recent checks suggest that Kinsus intends to bring
two more factories online, one in CY28 (k6c) and one in CY29 (k6d). This implies that
capacity would increase another ~25% in CY28 and ~20% in CY29, bringing Kinsus' total
ABF substrate capacity to almost double exiting CY29, relative to its CY26 capacity.

Ibiden (4062.T, covered by Shoji Sato)
Back in February, Ibiden first announced that it will invest capex of ¥500bn in the
electronics field planned through F3/29, for ABF substrate expansion. Sato-san's latest
view is that Ibiden will need a new factory building to install the equipment and facilities
that will be purchased with the ¥500bn. As a result, Sato-san expects Ibiden during its 2Q
results briefing (October 30) to announce plans to make a large capital investment,

including for a factory building with a long lead time, and raise its medium-term earnings
forecast (report here).
SEMCO (009150.KS, covered by Shawn Kim)
On September 29, SEMCO announced investment of KRW4.27 trillion (~US$3,178mn) in
Sejong and KRW2.51 trillion (~US$1,488mn) in Vietnam, for AI server semiconductor
substrate capacity expansion.
• Sejong: Investment period from September 2026 to May 2028, with mass
production starting in September 2028. Largest single-product investment to date,

serving as key hub for mass production of high-value-added, highly demanding
products, proactively responding to the increasing demand for AI server substrates.
• Vietnam: The Vietnamese subsidiary aims to complete construction and begin
operations by June 2030. It will be operated as a strategic hub to respond to global
volume demand and enhance production efficiency through a stable supply
system.
With this investment, Samsung Electro-Mechanics plans to actively respond to the

growing demand for high-value-added semiconductor substrates in high-growth markets
such as AI servers, HPC, and automotive electronics. In particular, this investment is being
pursued based on medium- to long-term cooperation with global big tech and
semiconductor customers. The investment includes customer funding support and
medium- to long-term volume guarantees; thus, the company expects to strengthen the
competitiveness of its FCBGA business based on a stable demand foundation.

(MOU) with Haiphong, Vietnam, for an investment to expand its semiconductor substrate

plant in Haiphong.
•  Construction will begin in July 2026 and is scheduled to be completed in May

• The site spans approximately 330,000 square meters (98,000 pyeong), equivalent

to 45 soccer fields.
• According to a news report, LG Innotek will invest approximately $1 billion, or
about 1.53 trillion won, for its Phase 1 expansion plan, which mainly includes RF-SiP
(radio frequency system-in-package) and FC-CSP (flip chip-chip scale package).
• ABF substrates will be part of the Phase 2 expansion plan, but no details or

initiated high-volume mass production of FC-BGA for PC CPUs in the third quarter of this
year. Simultaneously, the company is rapidly boosting revenue by securing additional
major clients. Starting next year, it plans to sequentially enter the AI accelerator and AI
server markets.
• Revenue from LG Innotek's package solution business has maintained an upward
trajectory for three consecutive years. With the package solution business

demonstrating tangible results alongside its optical solution business, the
company has strengthened its capacity to generate stable earnings.
• LG Innotek plans to rapidly expand its market share in the emerging semiconductor
substrate market, aiming to build its package solution business, including
semiconductor substrates, into a business generating US$743 million in operating
profit by 2031.
• In the future, LG Innotek plans to develop its future businesses, including

semiconductor substrates and physical AI solutions, to US$5.94 billion by 2030.
The company aims to have such future new businesses account for 25% of total
revenue, thereby completing a well-balanced business portfolio.
The expanded plant will produce major semiconductor substrates including radio
frequency system-in-package (RF-SiP), flip chip chip scale package (FC-CSP), and flip chip
ball grid array (FC-BGA). "The Gumi site will serve as a 'mother factory' responsible for

developing new semiconductor substrate technologies and producing new models and
high-value-added products, while the expanded plant in Vietnam will be operated as a
production base for general-purpose semiconductor substrates," an LG Innotek official
explained.

Daeduck announced on July 20, 2026, that it is investing ₩497 billion (~US$370M)
manufacturing facilities and related infrastructure for semiconductor products, running

through December 31, 2027.
ABF capex is stepping up, but announced investment still falls short of implied
demand. We estimate that ABF substrate makers have announced ~US$14.3bn of
investment year to date, materially above the segment’s aggregate annual capex in recent
years, although these commitments span multiple years. Our tally is not exhaustive. LG
Innotek has yet to disclose the size of its planned ABF capacity investment, while Shinko
has not quantified the additional spending required to expand capacity following its

announced acquisition of a JDI fab.
Our supply chain checks suggest that each US$1 of investment can support US$0.50–
0.60 of annual revenue at full ramp. On this basis, the US$14.3bn announced so far could
support US$7.2–8.6bn of incremental annual revenue. This compares with US$11.4bn of
incremental revenue implied by our industry forecast. We model the ABF substrate
market reaching US$20.9bn in 2030, up from US$9.5bn in 2026. Despite the step-up in

meet our demand forecast, pointing to a need for further capacity expansion over the

Morgan Stanley Research 13
The State of the Current Cycle
The ABF substrate market has gone through a few cycles over the past two
decades. We believe we are now still in the early innings of an up-cycle.
There was a prolonged down-cycle from 2012-18 as the PC TAM shrank amid the rise of
smartphones and tablets. During this tough period, companies were not able to generate
profits and had to compete on price to fill their idle capacity. As a result, during those

years, there was no or very limited capacity expansion, and capital expenditures by the
major ABF substrate suppliers were kept at a minimum.
Even as general server demand picked up, lifting utilization rates across the group going
into 2020, ABF substrate suppliers remained cautious on the longer-term demand outlook
and remained disciplined, hesitating to expand capacity. Hence, capex turned up just
slightly in 2019-20, increasing ~US$600mn vs. ~US$2.2bn on average per year in 2012-18.

This underinvestment led to sudden undersupply, as the shock from Covid-19 drove strong
work-from-home demand for PCs, as well as increased data usage and storage for general
computing driven by online entertainment. This ultimately led to a significant increase in
ABF substrate market value from 2020-22, reflected by the sudden surge in demand
along with meaningful pricing adjustments owing to the lack of supply.
During this 2020-22 period, much capacity expansion was set in motion as ABF substrate

suppliers and customers came to agree that necessary investments were needed for
future growth. Some subsidies were even given to these suppliers to facilitate this
capacity expansion. This is why capex jumped from ~US$2.9bn in 2020 to US$4.1bn in
2021 and US$5.5bn in 2022; even in 2023-24, capex remained elevated, as capacity
expansion plans continued.
Unfortunately, demand did not play out as expected. As the world came out of the
pandemic, demand for PCs and online entertainment fell sharply. This normalization of

demand, coupled with capacity expansion from practically every major ABF substrate
supplier, resulted in the ABF substrate market once again becoming oversupplied. This led
to a sharp decline in ABF substrate market value in 2023-24, as volumes normalized,
driving prices down to normalize against the new supply/demand dynamic.
Fortunately, cloud demand remained strong for general computing, and with AI demand
starting to pick up, the down-cycle this time around was much shorter (two years) vs. the

prior down-cycle (six years).
In 1H24, the ABF substrate market troughed and we saw an upward inflection since then,
but the pace of the recovery has been moderate and bifurcated. Midrange/low-end
markets such as PCs have shown moderate demand recovery, with high-end server and AI-
related end-demand recovering and growing at a faster pace. This is why we turned the
page on our bearish stance in 1H25, but did not turn more bullish until early 2026.

### Page 14

M
Idea
14
Periods Description
Period I (2010-2012): PC-centric
equilibrium
ABF substrate demand is primarily driven and dominated by CPU and GPU demand for desktops, notebooks,
discrete GPUs, with a meaningfully smaller exposure towards server CPUs.
Period II (2012-16): PC decline
and demand dilution
Global PC demand entered  a structural down-cycle as consumer behavior changed, with smartphones and tablets
absorbing more consumer time spent. This resulted in lower volumes and increased pricing competition as

suppliers fought to fill idle capacity.
Period III (2016-18): The quiet
inflection - servers and early
HPC
During these years, the PC market matured, while cloud computing started to take off. At the same time, server
CPU substrates quietly grew larger with more layers. We also started to see early accelerators being adopted in
high performance computing.
Period IV (2018-2022): WFH
demand driven structural
shortage
The Covid-19 pandemic drove massive work from home demand for consumer electronics and cloud computing.

This sudden surge in PC demand and increased time spent on gaming and online entertainment, bundled with a
continuous increase in substrate package size and layer count, resulted in a structural shortage, which also resulted
in longer lead times and raised prices. It was during this period (2021-22) that ABF suppliers globally started to
invest in new capacity.
Period V (2022-2024): Post-
pandemic correction and new
capacity arrival
As the world came out of the Covid-19 pandemic, WFH demand faded and  PC and cloud computing demand were

negatively affected. But at the same time, the new investments and capacity additions were just starting to slowly
come online, resulting in a drop in utilization rates and pricing.
Period VI (2024-2026):
Bifurcated recovery
PC demand normalized at the pre-Covid levels, with some replacement demand driven by Windows upgrade, while
the recovery and growth of AI and general server demand for cloud picks up at a meaningfully higher growth
trajectory.

Period VII (2026-30): AI diffusion Demand for AI proliferates from training to inference, driving ABF substrate market growth to re-accelerate over the
next five years after a two-year down-cycle following the Covid-19 pandemic. This drives increased demand for AI
computing, networking, and cloud computing, while demand for PCs remains soft.

Breaking down ABF substrate end demand
The end market mix for ABF substrates has shifted dramatically over the past decade. PCs

were once the dominant demand driver, accounting for approximately 70% of global ABF
substrate market value in 2015, supported primarily by PC CPUs and GPUs. Since then,
however, the industry's demand profile has undergone a structural transformation. We
estimate that PC-related applications represented only ~23% of total ABF substrate
market value in 2025, and this share is expected to decline further to below 10% by 2030.
In contrast, demand from servers, AI GPUs, AI ASICs, and networking applications has

expanded rapidly. We estimate that these segments collectively accounted for only
around 10% of the ABF substrate market in 2015, but their share increased to ~60% by
2025. Looking ahead, we expect they will represent more than 80% of total ABF
substrate market value by 2030.
In our view, this structural shift in end-market demand has been the primary driver of ABF
substrate industry growth over the past decade and will become even more pronounced

over the next five years. As AI infrastructure deployment accelerates, we expect continued
expansion in server, AI accelerator, and networking demand to drive meaningful
acceleration in ABF substrate market growth.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target increases ~21% to NT$1,775 (from NT$1,465), driven by our
increased earnings estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have
returned to a growth track from 2026 onward and will show stronger  growth in 2027,
driven by the high-end ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply and Unimicron's
dominant supply share in AI ASICs and also server CPUs, which puts it in a favorable

position to benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and see growth
accelerating from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs, and networking chips. New
technologies such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T should also increase the
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, which adds to the under-capacity as

usable output decreases.
Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value Unimicron – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm as it takes into account cost of equity. Our key assumptions
include:
• Cost of equity of 9.2% (unchanged)
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 14% (slightly lowered from 15%)

• Terminal growth rate of 3% (unchanged, similar to the rest of the companies under
our tech hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,030 and NT
$595, respectively.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~29%, to NT$2,000, reflecting our raised earnings
estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have returned to a growth
track from 2026 onward and will show stronger growth in 2027, driven by the high-end
ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply, NYPCB's dominant supply share for
networking chips, and its proactive pricing strategy, which puts it in a favorable position to

benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and growth should
accelerate from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs and networking chips. New
technologies, such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T, should also increase
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, adding to the overall undercapacity
as usable output decreases.

Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value NYPCB – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. Key assumptions
(all unchanged):
• Cost of equity of 9.3%;
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 17%  –  fair given our analysis of the substrate market

over the next couple of years;
• Terminal growth rate of 3% (similar to the rest of the companies under our tech
hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,575 and NT
$1,020, respectively.

▪ We expect the ABF substrate market to
revert to undersupply from 2027 onward,
with the undersupply gap reaching 26% by
2030.
▪ This is primarily driven by AI demand, with
next-gen AI/networking chips adopting
larger body size ABF substrates.
▪ In the near to medium term, the short
supply of T-glass will be a key watch point,
but we believe this will mainly affect low-

end ABF and BT substrates, as suppliers
prioritize supply to the AI chip vendors.
▪ Our price target implies 41x 2027e P/E or
24x 2028e P/E, above the 10-20x range at
which it traded in the previous up-cycle. We
deem this fair, given AI chip substrate spec
upgrades and suppliers' ability to raise
prices.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

MediaTek

ASIC business expanding into Bytedance; 
potential high-end mobile gains in ‘27

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 4,475 TWD

Bytedance expanding ASIC TAM beyond Google/xAI 
Bloomberg reported on October 15th that Bytedance is close to an agreement to work 
with Mediatek on the ASIC design and turnkey service support following its collaboration 
with Broadcom and Qualcomm. This is consistent with our update in September that 
Mediatek could co-develop an AI inference chipset with Bytedance from 1H27 for tape 
out in 1H28 and see contribution from production in early ’29. Similar to Mediatek and 
xAI’s collaboration, we believe Bytedance would need the support on sourcing 
components (e.g. HBM and substrate) in addition to wafer manufacturing/packaging, 
lifting the dollar content vs. Google’s TPUs. Assuming 1mn unit demand annually & 
US$20-25k ASPs including advanced logic dies and HBM, the project could contribute 
US$20-25bn to Mediatek’s sales in ’29 (vs. BofA sales expectation on Mediatek  of 
US$82bn in ’28 with US$45bn from Google TPUv8/v9 and US$9bn from xAI’s 1-2 
quarter contribution). 
A modest tailwind in mobile as it gains share at Samsung 
The mobile chipset builds from Mediatek and Qualcomm in 2H26 are tracking below 
seasonal in 2H26 as the end demand is impacted by the elevated memory cost. While 
the market outlook looms into ’27 (vs. -25% Android unit demand in ’26), we expect 
Mediatek’s mobile business to fare better especially in the high-end segment where it 
could gain unit share by penetrating into Samsung’s flagship smartphones historically 
dominated by Qualcomm and Samsung’s own SoCs. Factoring 30% share in Samsung’s 
high-end mobile shipment of 30-35mn annual units and US$200+ chipset ASPs, it could 
be ~20% accretive to Mediatek’s mobile sales and ~5% of its total business from 2H27. 
EPS and valuation downside limited after recent pullback 
We maintain our Buy rating & NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E (implied 30x ASIC 
EPS + 10x merchant EPS). The share has been dragged by TPU competition concern in 
the near term but could be lifted by expanding ASIC projects and high-end mobile gains.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

大立光(3008)

Target Price：7,800

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 
⚫ Largan 大立光電(3008.TT)公司簡介 
大立光電(3008.TT)成立於 1987 年，並於 2002 年台灣證券交易所
掛牌上市。大立光作為全球光學塑膠鏡頭領導廠商，主要業務內容
為研發與製造精密光學塑膠鏡頭，產品主要應用於智慧型手機，其
餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手
機品牌廠，主要競爭者則為玉晶光 (3406.TT) 及舜宇光學

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。 
展望未來，記憶體供需緊張預期至少延續至 2028 年，全球市場短

期仍難恢復成長 ， 預估2027 年出貨量將進一步 Y-2.9%至 10.19 億
支。區域及產品結構方面，新興市場因價格敏感度較高，入門及中
低階機種需求仍將承受較大壓力，而美國、英國等成熟市場則因高
階機種占比較高，受漲價影響相對有限。品牌競爭方面， Apple、
Samsung 及 Huawei 憑藉較強的供應鏈議價能力 、 零組件取得能力
及高階產品布局，在產業成本結構上升下相對有利，市場集中度可
望進一步提高。

⚫ 營運現況 
新機拉貨帶動營收季增，良率及綠電成本壓抑毛利表現 
大立光 3Q26 營業收入為 156.51 億元，Q+14.53%、Y-11.46%，
主要受惠新款 iPhone 進入備貨旺季，帶動高階鏡頭出貨增加。毛
利率為 42.21%，Q-7.21ppts、Y-4.99ppts，主要受到新機導入初期
良率較低，以及綠電採購成本增加影響。業外收益 13.57 億元，其
中包含匯兌收益 1.97 億元，支撐整體稅後獲利季增。綜合以上，大
立光 3Q26 營業收入為 156.51 億元，Y-11.46%、Q+14.53%，毛

利率 42.21%，Y-4.99ppts、Q-7.21ppts，營業利益率 30.25%，

⚫ 未來展望 
拉貨動能逐步趨緩，良率改善有望緩解毛利率壓力 
展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，
仍難以完全彌補既有訂單下修影響，加上目前客戶需求能見度偏
短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028
年才能反映於報價，預期短期毛利率改善幅度仍將受到限制。綜合
以上 ， 研究部預估大立光4Q26 營業收入為 155.21 億元 ，Y-9.86%、
Q-0.83%，毛利率 46.65%，Y-1.32ppts、Q+4.44ppts，營業利益
率 35.29%，Y-1.46ppts、Q+5.04ppts，稅後純益 54.91 億元，
Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026
年營業收入為 603.80 億元 ，Y-1.26%， 毛利率46.84%，Y-3.59ppts，
營 業 利 益 率 35.06% ，Y-3.47ppts ， 稅 後 純 益 214.67 億元，
Y+0.90%，EPS 為 164.12 元。

手機主鏡頭規格持續升級，高階光學產品支撐營運動能 
展望 2027 年，智慧型手機主鏡頭將有規格升級，帶動高階鏡頭設
計提升，目前相關產品已完成技術準備並持續送樣，有望隨新機導
入逐步貢獻營收。潛望式鏡頭方面，下一波較大幅度規格升級預計
落在 2028 年，目前已進入試產階段，2027 年則以既有產品出貨為
主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

第一金證券投資顧問股份有限公司(First Capital Management Inc.) 台北市長安東路一段 35 號 3F   TEL(02) 2563 -1666 
營業執照字號:114 金管投顧新字第 013 號 
5 
FA 預計 2H27 量產，精密對位與整合能力開啟光通訊新成長動能 
光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling
則持續開發 ， 預計2028 年後逐步導入 。 公司憑藉微米級精密對位、
自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大
立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元 ，Y+17.08%， 毛利率51.37%，
Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24
億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，
2027 年營運有望重回成長軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目
標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

Document 235: Allianz_2026-10-08_From legacy capital to smart bets Europe's path to a competitive auto industry_a5c640fb

Source PDF: all report pdf/Allianz_2026-10-08_From legacy capital to smart bets Europe's path to a competitive auto industry_a5c640fb.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

In Summary

• Chinese brands will dominate  the Paris auto show  next week . They represent 50% of 
exhibitors versus just 23% in 2024, a visible sign of a deeper, structural shift already 
reshaping the market.  China holds 10% of European registrations, 16% in battery electric 
vehicles (BEV) and 26% in plug-in hybrid electric vehicle s (PHEV). This comes as Europe's 
auto market defies a sluggish growth outlook, up +5% YTD through August, suggesting 
structural change rather than a cyclical blip. Low -carbon demand leads the charge: BEV 
sales are up nearly +45% YTD, reaching parity with petrol-powered engine s (22%), amid 
energy volatility, EV cost compression and targeted subsidies for low incomes. 
• A deeper rift is opening between political ambition and industrial execution, and it risks 
setting a technology trap. Easing targets now breeds complacency precisely when the shift 
to low-carbon production needs to accelerate. Europe is poised to soften carbon penalties 
and cut the 2035 zero -emission target to 90%, relieving Original Equipment Manufacturers 
(OEMs) who underinvested rather than forcing them to catch up. Support was needed to 
share the burden, but without a binding roadmap, automakers will simply buy time, falling 
further behind Chinese rivals. Demand itself is uneven : BEVs are today the second biggest 
segment in France and Germany (30 and 26%), yet lag below 10% in Italy, Spain or even in 
the key but less accessible US market, exposing a dangerous short -term versus long -term 
arbitrage. 
• Added-value erosion of EUR3bn by 2030 must be urgently contained. The shift in dominant 
technology and rising competition from China have eroded the big three German carmakers' 
market share materially in core markets – down 10pps in Europe and 7pps in China since 
2021. In Europe, where manufacturing costs are less competitive, domestic added-value per 
vehicle in H1 2026 was 30-45% lower than in 2021/2022 while in China that decline has been 
smaller, less than 20% over the same period. A further realistic 3pps market share slide in 
both markets by 2030 would translate into a EUR3bn added-value deterioration for German 
OEMs, equivalent to 4pps of EBITDA margin. 
• Whoever secures reliable, cost-competitive battery production will shape the next decade 
of automotive competitiveness . Vehicle value is migrating from the factory floor to the 
software stack: Engine and chassis once represented 35 –45% of an internal combustion 
engine (ICE) car's value, but battery and software now account for 50 –60% of a BEV's. 
"Made-in-Europe" content requirements would preserve some manufacturing jobs, but at a 
steep cost: ~ EUR2,000 per vehicle (+5% retail price), coverage of only ~50% of domestic 
battery demand by 2035 and a wider technology gap with global peers. They would do little 
to reduce sovereign risk either: over 70% of European-based battery technology will be held 
by Asian firms , according to the current pipeline . The more achievable path is to grow 
Europe's footprint in capital -light, high -value segments (design and IP ), capturing added 
value without the capital risk of manufacturing.  
• Europe should pursue smart partnerships over full sovereignty, anchoring Asian battery 
and chip production on European soil, pooling procurement and R&D and consolidating 
onto fewer EV platforms under strict capital discipline. Diversifying further into  into 
defense looks relevant it has strong public funding but is running out of capacity . The long-
term bets worth making are solid-state batteries, recycling, proprietary chip design, local AI 
infrastructure and autonomous trucking and logistics, where Europe can differentiate 
without competing head on against entrenched US and Chinese leaders.

Guillaume Dejean  
Senior Sector Adv isor 
guillaume.dejean@allianz -
trade.com

Ano Kuhanathan  
Head of Corporate Research  
ano.kuhanathan@allianz -
trade.com

Allianz Research | 8 October 2026 
From legacy capital to smart bets : Europe's path to 
a competitive auto industry

### Page 2

2

A legacy capital mindset behind a structural crisis 
The 2026 Paris auto show  is emblematic of the changing global order  in the automotive industry . European 
brands account for just 40% of exhibitors in this year’s edition of the flagship industry event , to be held from 12-18 
October, down from 68% compared to 2022 and 2024. This pullback reflects intense cost-cutting measures amid a 
severe profitability crisis, with manufacturers engaging in tactical arbitrage. Meanwhile, Chinese players are seizing 
the opportunity and confirming their strong interest in the European market: Chinese brands surged from 9% to 50% 
of exhibitors at the event, and Chinese OEMs from 15% to a dominant 63% by 2026, using this prime European stage 
to attract new audiences and accelerate their continental expansion. This highlights the rebalancing of power within 
the automotive industry, where dynamic and innovative new competitors are openly contesting the long -held 
dominance of Europe's established giants. While this is  not the industry's first downturn, this time the weakness is 
structural rather than cyclical, and its root is identifiable: a legacy capital mindset that, for two decades, funded the 
defense of mature ICE assets and short -term margins instead of the electric, digital and industrial bets that would 
determine the next cycle. The technology lag, the China shock and the brand erosion are best read not as separate 
failures but as symptoms of that same underlying capital allocation culture.

Figures 1 & 2 : Breakdown of attendees of Paris auto show by  origin of OEMS and brands  /  
12-month trailing revenue, profits and margin rate from largest German automakers

Sources: Salon de l’Auto notice, LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

A legacy capital mindset: capital defended the past, chased the quarter and bet small when it finally moved.  
European automakers' balance sheets tell the story of a capital culture built for a different era. For years, capital 
was steered to sustain and optimize mature ICE platforms and defend near -term margins – the sunk-cost logic of 
an incumbent reluctant to cannibalize its own cash cow – rather than to pre-fund the electric and digital transition 
while there was still runway to do it gradually. When bets were finally placed, they were sized and governed like 
legacy capex, not like venture-style industrial transitions: The ill-fated decision to internalize middleware software 
development, a domain far removed from automakers' core competencies, burned time and money for negligible 
results, while billions in diesel -related penalties and surging impairment charges drained c ash reserves that could 
instead have funded the transition of ICE plants to EV production lines, scaled battery gigafactories or fueled critical 
R&D. Historically, the auto sector led capex growth during expansion years but proved more vulnerable in 
downturns. Yet today's situation is uniquely alarming: The broader economic cycle remains steady while auto capex 
is collapsing ( -20%, its strongest contraction since 2018) even as non -auto European blue-chip capex expands by 
+10%. That is short-termism showing up directly in the numbers: capital retreating precisely when software-defined 
vehicles, AI -powered autonomous systems and the early diffusion of robotaxis are reshaping the industry's  
competitive frontier. Having already missed the EV wave and fallen behind in the software revolution, European 
carmakers cannot afford to sit out the autonomous race. Yet the pause on Level 3 development is another example 
of risk -averse, low -conviction capital deployment at exactly the wrong moment. In this industry, moats and

technology edges erode fast. Today's investments are tomorrow's profits, and a mindset built to defend yesterday's 
assets has left financial leeway too thin to allow another misjudgment.

The second structural weakness is a  late EV transition.  European OEMs didn't just misjudge the pace of 
electrification, they kept capital deployed to defend ICE production and margins for as long as the balance sheet 
allowed, leaving too little invested when the pivot could no longer be delayed. Electrification diffused faster than 
they bet on: BEVs now account for over 20% of EU registrations (on par with ICE this year) and nearly 50% in China, 
the fastest-growing segment on both continents, and European carmakers are scrambling to catch up. The late 
pivot from ICE to EV and hybrid production is now compressing costs into a brutal timeframe: what could have been 
a gradua l, capital -light transition is instead an expensive, chaotic restructuring. German OEM margins have 
collapsed from 13% in 2022 to roughly 4% today. BEV profitability remains structurally lower than ICE, while Chinese 
competitors deliver advanced technology  at aggressive price points. The same mindset shows up in how setbacks 
were funded: Diesel-gate alone cost Volkswagen over USD34bn, capital that was unavailable to fund a decade of 
electrification R&D instead. The traditional European advantage  – a deep, local supplier ecosystem built on 
mechanical engineering – is eroding fast as EV supply chains shift toward Asian battery and component makers. 
European OEMs may have caught up on sales volumes at home, but their cost structures remain bloated, their 
margins thin and their competitive moat narrower by the quarter.

The third structural weakness is competition from  China: the price of capital that protected margins instead of 
market share.  Over the past decade, China was the biggest and most profitable market for German OEMs, 
accounting for over 30% of group revenue , and capital allocation treated that profit pool as something to protect 
rather than a market worth fighting for with scale. That era is over as the balance of power has been fully disrupted 
by the rise of local players that quickly absorbed foreign brands' market share. The miscalculation was strategic as 
much as financial: European OEMs underestimated Beijing's electrification ambition and the speed at which 
domestic, and in some cases less-experienced, firms would scale, while committing far less capital to matching that 
scale than the threat warranted. Competition has been exported over the past years, with Chinese brands 
redirecting overcapacity mainly towards Europe as high tariffs have almost entirely closed access to the US market. 
Their EU market share surged from about 2% to nearly 10% by mid -2026, totaling over 1mn cars sold on the 
continent for the first time. EU tariffs of up to 35% barely slowed them: Chinese OEMs pivoted to hybrids and PHEVs, 
offered advanced tech at aggressive prices and adopted European -style design. The trade picture tells the rest. 
Europe's historical automotive surplus with China, once peaking near EUR20bn, has flipped into an unprecedented 
deficit. EU auto exports to China halved in two years. In the opposite direction, Chinese EV and hybrid exports to the 
EU doubled to USD14.2bn in H1 2026 alone, with hybrid shipments surging +206%. The deficit is no longer just about 
BEVs; it spans hybrids, PHEVs and components, reflecting the broader penetration of Chinese brands while the 
European footprint in China fades. There is no reason to believe this wave will recede in the near future as OEMs 
intensify relocation of some production capacities in Europe to dodge any trade barrier threat. In the meantime, 
much improvement has been mad e on the after-sales networks and client relationships that remained one of the 
last Achilles' heels of Chinese firms in Europe, and the value proposition is perfectly adapted to European customer 
demand: a good -looking and affordable advanced technology which is precisely the segment European capital 
under-invested in. 
Figures 6 & 7 : Share of imported Chinese brands in Europe vs. exported European brands in China / trade balance 
between EU-27 and China per powertrain breakdown (in EUR bn)

Sources:  Eurostat, CAAM, Allianz Research  
The fourth structural weakness:  Brand power fades once the capital behind the premium stops compounding.  
The aura of German automotive excellence, built on decades of engineering prestige and glossy design , was itself 
a form of capital, accumulated through sustained investment in mechanical engineering. It is cracking now that the 
industry pivots toward electrification and digitalization. The core value in a 2026 vehicle now sits in software-defined 
solutions and battery systems, both firmly controlled by Asian firms, leaving European carmakers defending a 
premium price tag no longer backed by technological superiority. Competitors, notably Chinese brands, deliver a 
sharper performance-to-price ratio, while consumer price sensitivity has surged since the 2022 energy crisis as car -
related costs (i.e. insurance, energy and maintenance) skyrocketed by 20-30% over the core inflation rate since 2020. 
The 2026 EV boom cannot be decoupled from volatile energy prices and aggressive price compression driven by 
fiercer competition and widening supply. This mirrors the broader luxury backlash: heritage alone no longer justifies 
the invoice when the capital that built it was never reinvested in the technology that now defines the category. 
Meanwhile, Europe's most popular segment (i.e. sedans and hatchbacks below EUR50,000) carries a 3 to 5% better 
price-to-range ratio than Chinese BEV models currently sold in Europe yet low export value as different consumer 
tastes and cheaper local alternatives in emerging markets cap revenue upside.  Brand awareness, once the

Not a premium purchase anymor e? EV adoption is increasingly breaking the psychological glass ceiling that has 
long confined electric cars to an affluent, early -adopter customer base.  The shift from broad -based subsidies 
towards targeted support is making EVs more accessible to mainstream households, while falling vehicle prices and 
improving total cost of ownership are progressively chasing away the perception of EVs as a premium goods. This 
virtuous circle is particularly important for the fleet market: As companies increasingly electrify their fleets, a 
growing volume of relatively young EVs will enter the second -hand market, expanding choice and bringing prices 
within reach of a much broader consumer base. This lifts one of the main structural barriers to adoption so far: the 
limited maturity of the used-EV market and the sharp depreciation suffered by first-generation models, which made 
residual values difficult to predict and leasing costs comparatively high. Energy-price volatility is another factor that 
adds up this year and strengthens the economic case.

Four industrial equations and the capital choices that solve them 
Solving the battery puzzle 
A must -have to support ongoing fleet electrification. As the automotive industry undergoes its most profound 
transformation in a century, batteries have become the undisputed centerpiece of the electric vehicle. Representing 
30–35% of a BEV's total value, no other single component carries as much influence on vehicle cost, range, 
performance and, ultimately, consumer adoption. This makes battery supply not just a procurement question but a 
strategic one – for OEMs, for governments and for Europe's industrial base at large. With BEV penetration rates 
climbing steadily to come close to a 100% share by 2035, the volume of cells required is set to grow exponentially. 
Whoever secures reliable, cost-competitive access to battery production will shape the next decade of automotive 
competitiveness. For Europe, carmakers cannot continue depending  on external suppliers for such a critical 
components. This is one of the keys to solving the profitability gap. 
The added-value of European-sold vehicles has depreciated by 30-45%  since 2021/2022 period. Looking at the 
top three German carmakers’ balance sheets and the annual evolution of car sales per market over the past years, 
we find that added-value per vehicle in H1 2026 declined by around -30% and -45% Europe since 2021/2022 period, 
while in China current added -value declined by -20% since the 2022 peak but is today almost equivalent to 2021 
level. Over this period we can observe a sharp decline of German OEM market share by 10% in Europe and 7% in 
China as the technology shift and consumer demand change reframe the market structure. Based on our 
calculations, we then estima te that a market share decline  of 50bp s in China and Europe , which would mean an 
annual deterioration of added-value by EUR480mn. A realistic continuous slide based on actual momentum by -3% 
market share in each market by 2030 would then account for a loss of around EUR3bn of added -value, or 4% of 
annualized 2026 EBITDA.

### Page 7

Self-autonomous production is not for today but can be considered tomorrow. The bloc has built enough pack -
assembly capacity to match domestic BEV and PHEV demand, yet production remains overwhelmingly foreign -
controlled: South Korean manufacturers alone operate roughly 65% of installed capacity, concentrated in heavier, 
shorter-lived nickel-ion chemistry (NMC). However, European production is less competitive compared to  Asian 
peers, and notably China. Due to a  poor resource base in lithium, cobalt and rare earths, a drastic labor cost 
differential (roughly 1 per 5) and initially weak domestic demand, European-branded cell production was recording 
thin-razor margins. Several projects collapsed under scale constraints even as order books were full. On this basis, 
there was not much choice  other than imports of foreign technology, hence a widening trade deficit in a strategic 
inputs that has sharply intensified over the past quarter as BEV demand in Europe increased massively. On 
batteries, the EU recorded a record high deficit last year of EUR47bn, which should be broken again this year based 
on current trends (EUR57bn as of July on an annualized basis). 
Chasing full supply-chain autonomy now is the wrong fight: Europe arrived too late and has too little runway to 
out-scale Chinese lithium-ion economics. Policy should pivot from building a sovereign chain to securing one, using 
instruments like the EU's localization rules (IAA -style content requirements) to anchor foreign capital and 
technology on European soil. Importing South Korean and, selectively, Chinese lithium -ion know -how is not 
capitulation; it preserves jobs, de -risks logistics and buys time, while accepting that near -term competitiveness in 
conventional cells is structurally out of reach.  It seems to be the path picked by European automakers as we have 
seen over the past month a multiplication of greenfield investment into European battery capacity from Chinese 
firms. Based on current public announcements, China’s pipeline expects an increase of capacity of roughly 150 GW 
by 2030, multiplying the country’s market share by more than three from 15% to 37% (and even 45% when including 
no-committed options).  
Europe should not confound industry support with sovereignty protection . On 7 October, Germany and France 
agreed to push for a further easing of the EU's 2035 car CO₂ targets – seeking an 80% reduction rather than the 
Commission's already softened 90% proposal – in exchange for "Buy European" local -content requirements on 
batteries sold in Europe. This deal comes alongside the EU's new EUR1.5bn Battery Booster Facility, designed to 
help European cell manufacturers ramp up production. Yet the two measures risk working at cross -purposes. 
Relaxing emission standards would inevitably slow EV adoption, shrinking the very demand that European battery 
makers need to achieve the scale economies required to compete. Europe's battery landscape remains 
overwhelmingly dominated by Asian players: current greenfield investment pipelines suggest South Korean and 
Chinese suppliers will command at least 70% of European market share by 2030 –2035, leaving homegrown firms 
with a modest 15–20%. By diluting the regulatory signal that underpins EV uptake, the Franco-German compromise 
may shield legacy automotiv e jobs in the short term, but it risks starving Europe's fledgling battery industry of the 
volumes essential to close the cost gap with entrenched Asian competitors – ultimately deepening, rather than 
reducing, the continent's technological dependence.

Regulation is a necessary defensive step but not the ultimate solution . The economics are blunt: we estimate 
Made-in-Europe content requirements add roughly EUR2,000, or about a 5% mark-up, to a battery pack's final price 
– a premium consumers absorb at precisely the moment BEV-ICE cost parity is meant to narrow, and when energy-
price volatility and tighter credit are already denting appetite for large purchases. The passing-through strategy is 
not timely and could be unfruitful as it could grip the scale-up pattern on demand. A quick look at domestic battery 
capacity pipeline in Europe back s up our point. Even under the most optimistic scenario (i.e. every announced and 
pipeline project realized, every factory running at full nameplate utilization ), European cell capacity will not cover 
all retail demand. In a more realistic approach – a path toward a BEV share at or near 100% by 2035 – we calculate 
that European capacity would cover barely half of retail demand by 2035. That is meaningful progress, but it is not 
self-sufficiency, and it leaves no realistic path to excluding Chinese technology without rationing consumer choice 
or inviting retaliation. The honest conclusion: sovereignty is worth pursuing, but only paced to what capacity actually 
allows. Europe cannot build from zero to one hundred, and has no time to try. The better bet is to use today's 
dependence as a bridge by protecting jobs and the ecosystem while demand scales and direct policy not at 
refighting yesterday's lithium -ion battle, but at owning the chemistries, solid -state and beyond that will define the 
next one.

Not doomsday, but the path forward is narrow.  Europe cannot afford to treat batteries as a secondary concern: 
EV traction is set to intensify under current mandates, and carbon-neutrality commitments are too consequential to 
let such a critical technology layer remain overwhelmingly dependent on foreign supply. But the response has to be 
realistic. Weaker cash reserves, thinner market support and tightening lending conditions are real investment 
constraints for European OEMs, and attempting to build a fully sovereign, vertically integrated battery industry from 
scratch is neither financially viable nor strategically sound, especially when domestic players cannot yet match the 
cost or technology benchmarks set by Asian champions; carrying that burden alone would divert capital from areas 
where Europe can genuinely differentiate. A EUR1bn battery factory inaugurated in Germany  in early October 
shows what a realistic middle path looks like: While cells continue to be sourced from China, the factory's true added 
value lies in its operational verticality (i.e. a full -stack deployment spanning R&D, design, battery pack 
manufacturing and recycling ). It demonstrates how supply -chain dependencies can be mitigated, even if fully 
eliminating them remains realistically unachievable.

### Page 9

In the short run, the sector should embrace smart partnerships, and turn recycling into a domestic feedstock.  
Strategic alliances with leading South Korean and Chinese cell manufacturers should be actively pursued but 
provided production is anchored on European soil to secure local employment, build industrial know -how and 
reduce supply-chain disruption risks . This should be alongside a pan -European procurement or spending -pooling 
mechanism that consolidates bargaining power and lowers unit costs without resorting to protectionism. In parallel, 
battery recycling should be scaled into a genuine domestic feedstock: recovery of lithium, cobalt and nickel from 
end-of-life packs is costly today, but as cell volumes scale with BEV penetration, unit economics should improve 
sharply, reducing reliance on imported raw inputs without requiring Europe to out-build Asian cell economics. 
In the longer run, bet on solid-state, the next chemistry generation. China's lead there remains unsettled even as 
it targets the technology in its latest five-year plan. Lithium is finite while Europe holds meaningful sodium 
reserves, and performance-per-lifespan economics should eventually undercut today's chemistries. This is the one 
battery front where Europe can compete on a reset map rather than catch up on an existing one. 
Balance the software vs hardware  
From engine to code line, the control center has shifted away. Europe has no choice but to take into consideration 
the new paradigm that is expected to intensify under a broader adoption of AI -empowered solutions and 
accelerated development of autonomous mobility tools. Though the leaders of automotive chips are mostly located 
in Europe, European-made production dramatically decline d in 40 years, down from 20% to hardly more than 5% 
this year. And Europe does not produce or design the leading -edge chips used for AI development (frontier model, 
robotics, software). The sharp decline in revenue and margins (slashed by 2.5x from its 2023 peak of over 20%) of 
Europe’s leading auto chipmakers is as much evidence of weak European industrial demand as of late deployment 
of domestic production supporting both AI -powered and autonomous mobility solutions. Today, European 
carmakers prefer using US technology and hardware due to their high -performance computing capacities. With 
Level 2+ autonomous features already accounting for roughly a third of new registrations this year, and forecast to 
surpass 70% by 2035, it is an addressable market of almost between 585 -650mn of vehicles over the 2026 -2035 
period.  If Europe's chip champions remain anchored to legacy power and analog niches while the car becomes a 
rolling data center, the continent risks permanent relegation in the technology race that will define the next decade 
of mobility.

Europe's software blind spot: the hidden dependency behind next -generation autonomous cars . Even the most 
advanced European car models , featuring L2+ driving assistance, AI -powered cockpits and cloud -connected 
services, rest on a technology stack overwhelmingly controlled by non -European players. As the architecture map 
of a leading European vehicle platform reveals, the critical layers , from semiconductors to AI assistants to cloud & 
data storage and mapping are sourced almost entirely from US and, for local market variants, Chinese providers. 
This is a structural exposure. Semiconductors represent the sharpest vulnerability, where Europe faces a persistent 
technology gap with US leaders like NVIDIA and Qualcomm, and no credible short -term substitute exists at the 
performance frontier required for autonomous driving  compute. Yet the picture is not entirely bleak. European 
OEMs have strategically retained in -house control over the operating system ensuring sovereignty over the core 
software layer that orchestrates all other functions. Co -development models on driving assistance stacks allow 
European players to maintain architectural oversight and avoid full dependency on licensed black-box solutions. IT 
infrastructure is similarly co-owned rather than fully externalized. On AI assistance, emerging European alternatives 
such as Mistral offer a credible path to reduce reliance on US hyperscalers. The imperative is clear: Europe cannot 
eliminate its dependency overnight, but it can , and must , deepen internal OS mastery, expand co -development 
leverage, nurture homegrown AI capabilities and treat semiconductor sovereignty as a decade -long strategic 
investment rather than a problem to be solved quarterly. 
Figure 17 : Current software and hardware infrastructure of most-advanced European L2+ car model

Sources:  Corporate public information, Allianz Research  
Software as a driver of revenue migration from mechanical to data -centered operations. The end -to-end car 
software market is set to grow from roughly USD340bn in 2025 to USD520bn by 2035, a sustained +10% CAGR, with 
the fastest growth concentrated in AI -driven and automation segments (i.e. software verification and validation 
services and software development itself ), outpacing hardware segments like control units  or sensors. The value 
chain is migrating from mechanical engineering to code, and with it comes a new revenue layer already visible in 
the market: over -the-air conduct and performance  features (heated seats, horsepower monitoring, acceleration 
boost), advanced automated driving assistance  (FSD) and usage -based insurance and predictive -maintenance 
services built on vehicle telemetry. These models convert a one -off hardware sale into recurring, higher -margin 
revenue, improving cash -flow visibility well beyond unit sales.  The diversification approach made by the US 
manufacturer Tesla could be an example as the services segment accounted for 16% of its total revenue (vs. 6% in 
2022) and offset a part of the decline of cash flow generated by the direct sales of cars that account for roughly 70% 
of its revenue against 85% at its peak in 2022.

Connected service value is locked up into recurrence : a 8% additional revenue share to not overlook . A simple 
simulation illustrates the point: assuming 1 mn vehicles sold per year over a decade, a EUR50/month subscription 
plan with consumer adoption gradually rising from 33% to 50%, cumulative recurring revenue reaches 
approximately EUR3bn by year 10 , translating into a roughly 70% CAGR from a modest ~EUR200mn base in year 
one. In context, that terminal run -rate would represent around 8% of the average 2025 annual revenue of top 
European carmakers, making it a meaningful diversification channel rather than a rounding error. Crucially, 
software-driven services carry structurally higher margins than vehicle manufacturing, and the EUR50 price point is 
conservative, as advanced features such as autonomous driving upgrades, predictive maintenance or in-car digital 
ecosystems mature, willingness to pay should rise in tandem. Once retention loops are proven and the service 
portfolio deepens, this segment has the potential to evolve from a supplementary income stream into a core profit 
engine as the business model is fully optimized. 
In the short run, firms should  secure a foothold in assembly, and stop duplicating R&D.  Full onshoring is neither 
realistic nor necessary: Building domestic chip assembly and packaging capacity , even on licensed foreign 
technology, as the battery segment demonstrates , is vastly preferable to total dependence on Asian production, 
provided a recalibrated public -private financing mix and faster infrastructure permitting remove the region's self -
inflicted red-tape deterrent. The same logic applies to R&D: high competition should not preclude partnership in 
non-core, pre-competitive domains. VW's Cariad experience , billions spent with limited scale , is a cautionary tale 
worth internalizing; Pooling R&D capital across OEMs to explore frontier technologies where no single player holds 
a decisive lead, then differentiating at the product and service layer, is a model already emerging in Japan that 
Europe should engage with, provided governance structures ensure ecosystem-wide benefit. 
Going forward, European automakers should own the chip IP, and treat software as the real upside. Europe won't 
close the semiconductor manufacturing gap, but it doesn't need to: Much like the GPU/CPU ecosystem where value 
accrues to designers rather than fabs alone, Europe should channel its engineering depth and thriving SME -AI 
ecosystem into proprietary, industry -specific chip design, particularly for next -generation autonomous vehic le 
architectures as the intellectual property moat matters more than the production line. The same shift in where value 
sits applies to software: the revenue and margin potential of data -driven services and physical -AI applications in 
vehicles is still largely unsized, yet t he digitalization of cars is an irreversible trend. Capturing that value requires 
reducing strategic dependence on US and Chinese hyperscalers, prioritizing local cloud and AI infrastructure would 
unlock economic returns while materially lowering disruption risk.

Getting ready for autonomous mobility 
Robotaxis are arriving in Europe , but r egulatory gates are opening gradually . Berlin's autonomous bus pilot 
alone spans a 2023 -2027 runway from planning to launch, with authorization landing only mid -2027, while China 
and the US have already built a 100+ city blueprint to borrow from. The bigger brake may be cultural: European 
approval of autonomous vehicles sits near 33%, against roughly 60% in China. That gap is narrowing, but slowly, 
and 2027 -2028 will s tay experimental rather than commercial . Even generously scaled, Europe's robotaxi fleet 
reaches only 300-600k vehicles by 2035, half the US, a third of China's, and capturing at best 17% of global volume 
in a bull case, 7% in base. Europe's approval architecture is consumer -protection-first, business-case second, and it 
shows in the timeline. Each extra approval layer compresses the payback window on capex -heavy hardware, and 
that asymmetry is precisely what deters a European manufacturing play: no domestic tech champion exists, entry 
costs are steep and the regulatory calendar guarantees Europe will always scale second. 
Figures 19 & 20  : Current and expected deployment of robotaxi services in city, per region breakdown / 
projections of European share of robotaxi distribution by 2035 (in volume)

Sources:  Corporate public filings, BCG
 , Counterpoint, Allianz Research

Robotaxis are the headline, but not the biggest or the smartest door into autonomy. Autonomous trucking is set 
to scale faster and bigger than robotaxis , a projected five-year CAGR of roughly +85% versus +50% for robotaxi s, 
with the truck segment reaching close to USD550bn by 2035 against robotaxi's USD420bn, while L3/L4 technology 
is still expected to represent under 9% of new car registrations that same year. That's a small, capital -intensive, 
already-concentrated market: China and the US dominate deployment  and the upfront fleet, and software costs 
are steep. A European OEM entering now would be underwriting high risk for thin, late-mover reward. The smarter 
play is backing adoption, not owning it , by partnering with established tech providers to secure distribution rights 
and data access across a market that will fragment into multiple channels (freight, transit, passenger) rather than 
building proprietary stacks from scratch. Robotaxis ’ real value to Europe are as a technology bridge: the same 
sensing and routing layers proven there translate directly into autonomous public transport, which aligns far better
